[187:34.16] put in b and x. So here the equation
[187:36.56] becomes y = to b1 x1 + b2 x2 + b3 x3 and
[187:41.04] so on till bn x n + c. So guys your
[187:44.96] equation of the straight line has the
[187:46.56] range from minus infinity to infinity.
[187:48.96] But in our case or you can say in
[187:50.48] logistic equation the value which we
[187:52.56] need to predict or you can say the y
[187:54.16] value it can have the range only from 0
[187:56.32] to 1. So in that case we need to
[187:58.32] transform this equation. So to do that
[188:00.80] what we had done we have just divide the
[188:02.72] equation by 1 - y. So now if y is equ=
[188:05.76] to 0. So 0 1 - 0 which is equals to 1.
[188:09.12] So 0 / 1 is again 0. And if we take y =
[188:12.96] 1 then 1 / 1 - 1 which is 0. So 1 / 0 is
[188:16.80] infinity. So here my range is now
[188:18.72] between 0 to infinity. But again we want
[188:21.36] the range from minus infinity to
[188:23.20] infinity. So for that what we'll do
[188:25.20] we'll have the log of this equation. So
[188:27.12] let's go ahead and have the logarithmic
[188:28.88] of this equation. So here we have just
[188:30.96] transformed it further to get the range
[188:32.80] between minus infinity to infinity. So
[188:35.28] over here we have log of y / 1 - 1 and
[188:38.40] this is your final logistic regression
[188:40.24] equation. So guys don't worry you don't
[188:42.16] have to write this formula or memorize
[188:44.08] this formula in Python. You just need to
[188:46.24] call this function which is logistic
[188:47.84] regression and everything will be
[188:49.28] automatically for you. So I don't want
[188:51.20] to scare you with the maths and the

[556:59.52] u, v and w. And this is nothing but our
[557:03.68] matrix over here. So this would be a of
[557:06.08] 0, a of 1, a of 2, a of three and

[557:20.64] So this is how a recurrent neural
[557:22.56] network would actually work. All right.
[557:25.36] So now that we know how RNN works, let
[557:27.68] us now understand what is LSTM or we can
[557:30.40] also say it as long-term memory. You see
[557:33.12] traditional RNNs are not good at
[557:35.04] capturing long range dependencies. What
[557:37.36] I mean to say here is that when we tend
[557:39.12] to work with a very huge data set and
[557:41.20] multiple RNN layer, we are at the risk
[557:43.44] of vanishing gradient problem. Now you
[557:45.60] might be wondering what is this
[557:46.80] vanishing gradient, right? Well, you see
[557:49.12] when training a very deep neural
[557:50.88] network, gradient or the derivatives
[557:52.96] decrease exponentially as it propagates
[557:55.04] down the layer. This is known as
[557:56.64] vanishing gradient problem. These
[557:58.64] gradients are actually used to update
[558:00.64] the weights of a neural network. But
[558:02.48] when the gradients vanish, these weights
[558:04.72] will not get updated. In the worst case
[558:06.96] scenario, it will completely stop the
[558:08.72] neural network from training. This
[558:10.48] vanishing gradient problem is a common
[558:12.40] issue in very deep neural networks. So
[558:15.04] to overcome this vanishing gradient
[558:16.72] problem in RNN's, long short-term memory
[558:19.36] was introduced. You see, LSTM or
[558:22.08] longshot memory is a modification to
[558:24.16] RNN's hidden layer. LSTM is capable of
[558:27.04] remembering RNN's weights and their
[558:28.96] inputs over a very long period of time.
[558:31.04] In LSTM, in addition to the hidden
[558:33.12] state, cell state is passed down to the
[558:35.12] next block. The way LSTM works is that
[558:37.60] it can capture long range dependencies
[558:40.16] that is old weights. It can have memory
[558:42.32] of previous inputs for a very extended
[558:44.40] time duration. The way LSTM cell does
[558:46.88] this is by using three main gates. First
[558:49.20] one is a forget gate. Forget gate
[558:51.36] removes the information that is no
[558:52.96] longer useful in the cell state. Then we
[558:55.44] have input gate. Additional information
[558:57.60] to the cell state is added by input
[558:59.68] gate. And finally we have something
[559:01.44] called as output gate. Additional useful
[559:03.60] information to the cell state is also
[559:05.60] added by an output gate. This gating
[559:08.08] mechanism of LSTM has allowed network to
[559:10.56] learn the conditions for when to forget,
[559:13.04] ignore or keep information in the memory
[559:15.12] cell. So let me now quickly move to my
